I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing I’m supposed to say here.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you’re experiencing something that others don’t seem to notice, or believing something strongly that others say isn’t true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the sense of, like, seeing or hearing things other people don’t. And I don’t think I have beliefs that people are telling me are untrue.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck on a feeling sometimes, but I know that’s different from what you’re asking, I think. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that people at work might be trying to harm you or undermine you in some way, even if there’s no clear evidence for that?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think they’re trying to harm me, no. Or undermine me, exactly.\n\nI do worry sometimes that I’m being judged or that I’m not measuring up, but I can usually tell that’s more my own worry than, like, evidence someone is plotting against me. I haven’t had a clear reason to think anyone is actually out to get me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs or ideas that you’re very certain about, even when others tell you they aren’t true or seem strange?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I can be pretty convinced of things about myself sometimes, like that I’ve messed something up, but if someone points out evidence otherwise, I can usually see that.\n\nI don’t think I have beliefs that other people would say are strange or not real. At least… not that I’m aware of. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms, like pain or other bodily sensations, that you haven’t been able to explain and that have been bothering you a lot?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I get normal aches and tension sometimes, but nothing that feels unexplained or that I’m really preoccupied with.\n\nI don’t think there’s been pain or bodily sensations that are bothering me a lot. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying a lot more than usual about things at work, to the point where it's hard to stop thinking about them, even when you try?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that is part of why I’m here. I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know work stress is normal, but lately I can’t really shake this feeling that work isn’t safe for me anymore.\n\nAnd it’s not just the job itself… it’s like I keep thinking I’m not the same capable person who got promoted. Like something in me is… damaged now, or not working right. I know that sounds kind of dramatic, maybe, but it gets stuck in my head and I have a hard time turning it off. Is that the kind of worrying you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing persistent anxiety specifically about your performance at work, such as constant worry about making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s less like one specific mistake I’m worried about and more this constant feeling that I’m not capable in the way I used to be.\n\nLike, I was promoted because I was good at my job, and now I keep feeling like that person isn’t really there anymore. I can’t shake the thought that work isn’t safe for me now, and that maybe I’m… damaged somehow. I know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what keeps running through my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar worries about your abilities or performance in other areas of your life, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s centered around work, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut… that feeling has sort of spilled over a little, like I don’t trust myself the same way. The main thought is still that work doesn’t feel safe anymore, and that I’m somehow damaged now—like the competent person who got promoted isn’t really there anymore.\n\nI’m not sure if that answers the question exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things the way you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big loss of interest, exactly. I mean, I’ve been more preoccupied, so maybe I’m not as present with things, but I wouldn’t want to say I don’t enjoy anything anymore.\n\nMaybe I’d need to think about it more… is that okay as an answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble functioning the way you normally would in your daily life, like taking care of responsibilities at home or interacting with friends and family?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean… home stuff is mostly okay, I think, but work has definitely not been like it used to be.\n\nI’ve missed some migration deadlines, and I’ve avoided being part of a couple of the later deployments that I normally would’ve handled. And I did get called out in a performance review for conflicts, which was… embarrassing, because that didn’t used to be me.\n\nI’ve also kind of stepped back from mentoring and some women-in-tech leadership things. I don’t feel very steady or reliable in those spaces right now, if that makes sense. I’m trying not to make excuses, but it has affected how I’m functioning.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling that something is off or that you're not the same started after a specific event or situation at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it did. It started after the break-in during that overnight deployment.\n\nI don’t know if it sounds strange that it’s still affecting me, but that was about two years and four months ago, and since then I just haven’t felt quite like myself at work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know work stress is normal, but lately I can’t really shake this feeling that work isn’t safe for me anymore.\n\nAnd it’s not just the job itself… it’s like I keep thinking I’m not the same capable person who got promoted. Like something in me is… damaged now, or not working right. I know that sounds kind of dramatic, maybe, but it gets stuck in my head and I have a hard time turning it off. I can’t shake the thought that work isn’t safe for me now, and that maybe I’m… damaged somehow. I know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what keeps running through my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar worries about your abilities or performance in other areas of your life, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s centered around work, I think. Is that the kind of worrying you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing persistent anxiety specifically about your performance at work, such as constant worry about making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s less like one specific mistake I’m worried about and more this constant feeling that I’m not capable in the way I used to be.\n\nLike, I was promoted because I was good at my job, and now I keep feeling like that person isn’t really there anymore. I can’t shake the thought that work isn’t safe for me now, and that maybe I’m… damaged somehow. I know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what keeps running through my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar worries about your abilities or performance in other areas of your life, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s centered around work, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut… that feeling has sort of spilled over a little, like I don’t trust myself the same way. The main thought is still that work doesn’t feel safe anymore, and that I’m somehow damaged now—like the competent person who got promoted isn’t really there anymore.\n\nI’m not sure if that answers the question exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things the way you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big loss of interest, exactly. I mean, I’ve been more preoccupied, so maybe I’m not as present with things, but I wouldn’t want to say I don’t enjoy anything anymore.\n\nMaybe I’d need to think about it more… is that okay as an answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble functioning the way you normally would in your daily life, like taking care of responsibilities at home or interacting with friends and family?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean… home stuff is mostly okay, I think, but work has definitely not been like it used to be.\n\nI’ve missed some migration deadlines, and I’ve avoided being part of a couple of the later deployments that I normally would’ve handled. And I did get called out in a performance review for conflicts, which was… embarrassing, because that didn’t used to be me.\n\nI’ve also kind of stepped back from mentoring and some women-in-tech leadership things. I don’t feel very steady or reliable in those spaces right now, if that makes sense. I’m trying not to make excuses, but it has affected how I’m functioning.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling that something is off or that you're not the same started after a specific event or situation at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it did. It started after the break-in during that overnight deployment.\n\nI don’t know if it sounds strange that it’s still affecting me, but that was about two years and four months ago, and since then I just haven’t felt quite like myself at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any strange or unusual perceptions, like seeing, hearing, or feeling things that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the sense of seeing or hearing things other people don’t.\n\nSometimes I’m on edge and I might misread a noise or get startled, but I wouldn’t say I’m perceiving things that aren’t there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong, unshakable belief that something was wrong with your work performance or your abilities—something that you were absolutely sure about, even if others tried to reassure you or show you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe not “absolutely sure” in a logical way, because I know people have tried to reassure me. But it’s hard to shake.\n\nA big part of why I’m here is that I keep feeling like work isn’t really safe anymore, and like something in me got… damaged, I guess. Like the competent person who got promoted and could handle things isn’t fully there now.\n\nI know that probably sounds dramatic. I’m not trying to be unfair to myself, but that belief just kind of sits there, even when I try to talk myself out of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like something in me is… damaged now, or not working right. I know that sounds kind of dramatic, maybe, but it gets stuck in my head and I have a hard time turning it off. Is that the kind of worrying you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing persistent anxiety specifically about your performance at work, such as constant worry about making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s less like one specific mistake I’m worried about and more this constant feeling that I’m not capable in the way I used to be.\n\nLike, I was promoted because I was good at my job, and now I keep feeling like that person isn’t really there anymore. I can’t shake the thought that work isn’t safe for me now, and that maybe I’m… damaged somehow. I know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what keeps running through my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar worries about your abilities or performance in other areas of your life, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s centered around work, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut… that feeling has sort of spilled over a little, like I don’t trust myself the same way. The main thought is still that work doesn’t feel safe anymore, and that I’m somehow damaged now—like the competent person who got promoted isn’t really there anymore.\n\nI’m not sure if that answers the question exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things the way you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean… home stuff is mostly okay, I think, but work has definitely not been like it used to be.\n\nI’ve missed some migration deadlines, and I’ve avoided being part of a couple of the later deployments that I normally would’ve handled. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong, unshakable belief that something was wrong with your work performance or your abilities—something that you were absolutely sure about, even if others tried to reassure you or show you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe not “absolutely sure” in a logical way, because I know people have tried to reassure me. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that people at work might be trying to harm you or undermine you in some way, even if there’s no clear evidence for that?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think they’re trying to harm me, no. Or undermine me, exactly.\n\nI do worry sometimes that I’m being judged or that I’m not measuring up, but I can usually tell that’s more my own worry than, like, evidence someone is plotting against me. I haven’t had a clear reason to think anyone is actually out to get me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any unusual beliefs or ideas that you’re very certain about, even when others tell you they aren’t true or seem strange?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying a lot more than usual about things at work, to the point where it's hard to stop thinking about them, even when you try?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that is part of why I’m here. I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know work stress is normal, but lately I can’t really shake this feeling that work isn’t safe for me anymore.\n\nAnd it’s not just the job itself… it’s like I keep thinking I’m not the same capable person who got promoted. Like something in me is… damaged now, or not working right. I know that sounds kind of dramatic, maybe, but it gets stuck in my head and I have a hard time turning it off. Is that the kind of worrying you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing persistent anxiety specifically about your performance at work, such as constant worry about making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I can’t shake the thought that work isn’t safe for me now, and that maybe I’m… damaged somehow. The main thought is still that work doesn’t feel safe anymore, and that I’m somehow damaged now—like the competent person who got promoted isn’t really there anymore.\n\nI’m not sure if that answers the question exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things the way you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms, like pain or other bodily sensations, that you haven’t been able to explain and that have been bothering you a lot?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I get normal aches and tension sometimes, but nothing that feels unexplained or that I’m really preoccupied with.\n\nI don’t think there’s been pain or bodily sensations that are bothering me a lot. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself worrying a lot more than usual about things at work, to the point where it's hard to stop thinking about them, even when you try?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that is part of why I’m here. I try not to make too big a deal of it, because I know work stress is normal, but lately I can’t really shake this feeling that work isn’t safe for me anymore.\n\nAnd it’s not just the job itself… it’s like I keep thinking I’m not the same capable person who got promoted. Like something in me is… damaged now, or not working right. I know that sounds kind of dramatic, maybe, but it gets stuck in my head and I have a hard time turning it off. Is that the kind of worrying you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing persistent anxiety specifically about your performance at work, such as constant worry about making mistakes or not meeting expectations?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s less like one specific mistake I’m worried about and more this constant feeling that I’m not capable in the way I used to be.\n\nLike, I was promoted because I was good at my job, and now I keep feeling like that person isn’t really there anymore. I can’t shake the thought that work isn’t safe for me now, and that maybe I’m… damaged somehow. I know that probably sounds harsh, but that’s kind of what keeps running through my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced similar worries about your abilities or performance in other areas of your life, not just at work?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s centered around work, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut… that feeling has sort of spilled over a little, like I don’t trust myself the same way. The main thought is still that work doesn’t feel safe anymore, and that I’m somehow damaged now—like the competent person who got promoted isn’t really there anymore.\n\nI’m not sure if that answers the question exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re not enjoying things the way you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’ve noticed a big loss of interest, exactly. I mean, I’ve been more preoccupied, so maybe I’m not as present with things, but I wouldn’t want to say I don’t enjoy anything anymore.\n\nMaybe I’d need to think about it more… is that okay as an answer?\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble functioning the way you normally would in your daily life, like taking care of responsibilities at home or interacting with friends and family?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean… home stuff is mostly okay, I think, but work has definitely not been like it used to be.\n\nI’ve missed some migration deadlines, and I’ve avoided being part of a couple of the later deployments that I normally would’ve handled. And I did get called out in a performance review for conflicts, which was… embarrassing, because that didn’t used to be me.\n\nI’ve also kind of stepped back from mentoring and some women-in-tech leadership things. I don’t feel very steady or reliable in those spaces right now, if that makes sense. I’m trying not to make excuses, but it has affected how I’m functioning.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling that something is off or that you're not the same started after a specific event or situation at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it did. It started after the break-in during that overnight deployment.\n\nI don’t know if it sounds strange that it’s still affecting me, but that was about two years and four months ago, and since then I just haven’t felt quite like myself at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any strange or unusual perceptions, like seeing, hearing, or feeling things that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the sense of seeing or hearing things other people don’t.\n\nSometimes I’m on edge and I might misread a noise or get startled, but I wouldn’t say I’m perceiving things that aren’t there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong, unshakable belief that something was wrong with your work performance or your abilities—something that you were absolutely sure about, even if others tried to reassure you or show you otherwise?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe not “absolutely sure” in a logical way, because I know people have tried to reassure me. But it’s hard to shake.\n\nA big part of why I’m here is that I keep feeling like work isn’t really safe anymore, and like something in me got… damaged, I guess. Like the competent person who got promoted and could handle things isn’t fully there now.\n\nI know that probably sounds dramatic. I’m not trying to be unfair to myself, but that belief just kind of sits there, even when I try to talk myself out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any perceptions, like seeing, hearing, or feeling things that others don't seem to notice, or have you ever had beliefs that you were absolutely sure about but others said weren't true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I can get tense and second-guess myself, but I haven’t had anything like seeing or hearing things other people don’t.\n\nAnd with beliefs… I have worries that feel really strong sometimes, but I don’t think I’m absolutely sure in a way where I can’t consider what other people are saying. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you were absolutely certain something was real or true, even when others said it wasn’t, and you couldn’t be convinced otherwise—like something you deeply felt in your gut was happening, even if it didn’t make sense logically?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had that, no. I can feel really convinced emotionally that something is wrong, but if someone talks it through with me, I can usually at least see that maybe I’m reacting from fear or stress.\n\nSo… no, not something where I was completely impossible to convince otherwise. I hope that’s answering it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so certain that something was wrong with your abilities or that work wasn’t safe for you, that even when people gave you evidence or reasons to think otherwise, you still couldn’t shake the feeling—like it was something you just knew deep down, even if it didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s closer to what it feels like. I don’t want to overstate it, because I know people have told me I’m doing okay or that I’m still capable.\n\nBut I’m here because I really can’t shake this feeling that work isn’t safe for me anymore, and that something in me got damaged somehow. Like the person who got promoted and knew what she was doing… isn’t really fully there now.\n\nI know that may not make perfect sense, and I can hear reasons against it, but deep down it still feels true. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this belief—that something in you is damaged and work isn’t safe anymore—feels completely unshakable to you, even when there’s clear evidence that you're still capable, and does it stand out from other worries you’ve had before in how certain you feel about it?\n\nPatient: I… yes, mostly. I don’t know if I should say “completely” unshakable, because that sounds so extreme, and I can understand when people point out that I’ve handled things before or that I was promoted for a reason.\n\nBut it still doesn’t really move the feeling. I’m here because I can’t shake it—that work isn’t safe for me anymore, and that I’m somehow damaged now. Like the competent person who got promoted isn’t really there anymore.\n\nAnd yes, it does feel different from my normal worries. It feels more… deep down true, even when I wish it didn’t. Is that okay to say?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
